                                    AFFIDAVIT OF CHRIS LEWIS
                                      Sworn December 11, 2024

             I, CHRIS LEWIS, of the City of Calgary, in the Province of Alberta, MAKE OATH
AND SAY THAT:

1.           I am the sole Director, Executive Chairman, and Chief Executive Officer of Cleo Energy
             Corp. (“Cleo”). As such I have personal knowledge of the matters herein deposed to, except
             where stated to be based upon information and belief, in which case I verily believe same
             to be true.

2.           I am authorized to swear this Affidavit as corporate representative of Cleo.




65560193\3
Rev. 2.0 (15/11/10)
                                                  -2-


3.      In preparing this Affidavit, I consulted with Cleo’s management and their legal, financial
        and other advisors. I also reviewed Cleo’s business records relevant to these proceedings
        and have satisfied myself that I am possessed of sufficient information and knowledge to
        swear this Affidavit.

4.      All references to dollar amounts contained herein are to Canadian Dollars unless otherwise
        stated.

RELIEF REQUESTED

5.      This Affidavit is sworn in support of an Application by Cleo for Orders under the
        Bankruptcy and Insolvency Act, RSC 1985, c B-3, as amended (the “BIA”) among other
        things:

        (a)       abridging the time for service of notice of this Application, deeming service of
                  notice of this Application to be good and sufficient, and declaring that there is no
                  other person who ought to have been served with notice of this Application;

        (b)       authorizing and directing the Royal Bank of Canada (“RBC”) to cancel any
                  cheques it issued as a result of the Garnishee Summons (as defined herein) and to
                  make available the Garnished Funds (as defined herein) into Cleo’s bank account
                  with RBC ending in 041 (the “Account”); and

        (c)       such further and other relief as Cleo may request and this Honourable Court may
                  grant.

DESCRIPTION OF THE CLEO’S BUSINESS AND BACKGROUND

6.      Cleo was originally incorporated in the Province of Alberta on May 31, 2016. Cleo is a
        wholly owned subsidiary of Chimera Management Group Ltd., a holding corporation.

7.      Cleo is a privately owned oil and gas operator of medium gravity oil based in Calgary,
        Alberta with operations throughout East Central Alberta. Cleo has been operating since its
        inception in 2016.




65560193\3
                                                 -3-


8.      Cleo has a number oil wells and gas wells located primarily in the following fields
        throughout the Western Canada Sedimentary Basin in East Central Alberta (the “Fields”),
        including at the following locations:

        (a)    Silver Heights;

        (b)    Shorncliffe;

        (c)    Neutral Hills;

        (d)    Neutral Hills North;

        (e)    Fabyan;

        (f)    Sedgewick.

9.      Cleo transports the oil and gas product (the “Products”) from the Fields to a local sales
        point either by pipeline or transported by trucks. The Products are then sold to a marketer
        and the revenue is paid out to Cleo on the 25th of every month.

EVENTS LEADING TO CLEO’s CURRENT CIRCUMSTANCES

10.     Cleo’s revenue is linked to the productivity of its oil and gas wells, as well as the market
        price of oil and gas. Cleo has recently experienced low production, partly due to
        mechanical failures and subsequently a major theft in its main producing oil and gas fields.

11.     Cleo’s financial difficulties further stem from a variety of factors, including the lack of
        capital investment to undertake necessary mechanical repairs which has led to the
        shutdown of the Cleo’s main producing fields contributing to a decline in production and
        revenue rates. Additionally, there has been a significant reduction in oil and gas prices
        during the month of September 2024, which has exacerbated Cleo’s current financial
        difficulties. These challenges have placed considerable strain on Cleo, causing monthly
        revenues to decrease to unsustainable levels.

12.     Despite Cleo’s efforts to reach profitability, Cleo continues to face financial difficulties.
        Cleo is unable to meet its obligations as they become due and is insolvent.


65560193\3
                                                -4-


13.     On December 8, 2024, Cleo filed a Notice of Intention to Make a Proposal (“NOI”)
        pursuant to subsection 50.4(1) of the BIA. Alvarez & Marsal Inc. has been appointed as
        proposal trustee (the “Proposal Trustee”). A copy of the NOI is attached as Exhibit “A”.
        Cleo filed the NOI in order to protect Cleo`s assets, maintain the value of its business and
        assets, protect itself and its stakeholders interests, and provide itself an opportunity and
        breathing space to restructure.

14.     Cleo has approximately $23,000,000 in debt, of which approximately $5,000,000 is
        secured. Further details relating to Cleo’s insolvency will be set out in a substantive
        affidavit being prepared for a stay extension hearing before the Honourable Justice Lema
        on January 6, 2024.

GARNISHEE SUMMONS

15.     I am advised by Cleo’s legal counsel Sam Gabor of Gowling WLG (Canada) LLP
        (“Gowling”), that as a result of the filing of the NOI, all enforcement proceedings against
        Cleo and its assets are automatically stayed for an initial period of thirty (30) days from
        December 8, 2024 (the “Stay”). Further, no new enforcement proceedings may be
        commenced or continued as against Cleo during the Stay, including the continued
        enforcement of garnishee summons.

16.     Garnishee proceedings were initiated against Cleo by Morganick Blending Services Corp.
        (“Morganick”) under Court File No. 2401-10333 prior to the Stay. Morganick’s collection
        agency T&S Collections Ltd. issued an initial garnishee summons (the “Initial
        Garnishment”) upon RBC on or around August 6, 2024 in the amount of $16,410.99,
        which amount was paid into Court by RBC. Attached as Exhibit “B” is an email from
        RBC confirming its reception of the Initial Garnishment. Attached as Exhibit “C” is the
        Certificate of Default Judgment obtained by Morganick.

17.     Morganick was not paid in full from the Initial Garnishment as a result of the Court’s
        allocation of funds between writ holders registered in the personal property registry
        (“PPR”). Accordingly, on or around November 11, 2024 Morganick’s collection agency
        caused a second garnishee summons (the “Garnishee Summons”) to be issued upon RBC



65560193\3
                                                 -5-


        in the amount of $152,436.18 in order to trigger payout of all writs registered in the PPR
        against Cleo and in order to cause Morganick to be paid in full by the Garnishee Summons.
        Attached as Exhibit “D” is an email from RBC confirming its receipt of the Garnishee
        Summons.

18.     Attached hereto and marked as Exhibit “E” is a recent PPR name search of Cleo
        evidencing the writ filed by Morganick as against Cleo.

19.     The Garnishee Summons caused the Account to be frozen by RBC and RBC to issue a
        cheque to the Court in the amount of $152,436.18 (the “Garnished Funds”).

20.     On December 9, 2024, a letter was sent by Gowling on behalf of Cleo to the Alberta Court
        of King’s Bench Accounting requesting that any garnished funds paid into court with
        respect to Cleo, including the Garnished Funds, need to be provided to Gowling as a result
        of the Stay. A copy of the letter is attached as Exhibit “F”.

21.     On December 9, 2024, a letter was sent by Gowling on behalf of Cleo to Morganick
        advising it of the NOI and Stay, and that the Garnished Funds would need to be repaid to
        Cleo. A copy of this letter is attached as Exhibit “G”. Gowling has also provided same
        letters to two other writ holders who have issued recent garnishments against Cleo advising
        them of the Stay.

22.     As a result of the recent Canada Post strike, the cheque sent by RBC for the Garnished
        Funds has not yet reached the Court. On December 11, 2024, I contacted the Court Registry
        which confirmed same to me.

23.     Gowling by letters requested of RBC on December 9, 2024 and December 11, 2024 that it
        unlock the Account, cancel the cheque for the Garnished Funds and make available the
        Garnished Funds into the Account as a result of the Stay. Copies of these letters are attached
        as Exhibit “H” and Exhibit “I”.

24.     RBC has unlocked the Account and Cleo now has access to it. However, the Garnishment
        Funds have not been returned into the Account by RBC presumably as a result of the
        Garnishee Summons. The Garnished Funds are needed by Cleo to meet its payroll


65560193\3
                                                 -6-


        obligations due this Friday, December 13, 2024 and to maintain business operations during
        the Stay. Cleo has twelve employees and nine consultants which fall under its payroll
        calculations. Cleo’s payroll obligations are approximately $132,000.

25.     Jason Enns of RBC, Cleo’s Private Banker, has requested a court order be provided to RBC
        for RBC to cancel the cheque for the Garnished Funds andmake the Garnished Funds
        available in the Account. Mr. Enns has advised me that RBC will follow a court order in
        this regard.

26.     If Cleo is unable to meet its payroll obligations and continue its operations during the Stay,
        its oil and gas assets will be placed into jeopardy creating potential environmental issues
        and risk of significant loss to Cleo and its creditors which are owed in excess of
        $23,000,000. As of the signing of this Affidavit, Cleo in good faith, is currently seeking
        interim financing from various sources and a prepayment of its oil revenues from its oil
        and gas marketer, however, none have yet confirmed that they will provide the respective
        financing or the prepayment by the end of the week. Accordingly, Cleo currently has no
        other confirmed adequate sources of available funds this week to meet its payroll
        obligations and Cleo is reliant on the Garnished Funds to do so.

27.     Cleo needs to make payroll to be in a position to maintain its oil and gas assets, and needs
        to continue carrying on business during the Stay to be able to proceed forward with the
        NOI proceeding for the benefit of its stakeholders.

28.     The Proposal Trustee is supportive of this application.

CONCLUSION

29.     I swear this Affidavit in support of an Application for the relief set out in paragraph 5 of
        this Affidavit and for no other or improper purpose.

Dear Sir/Mesdames:

Re: In the Matter of the Notice of Intention to Make a Proposal of Cleo Energy Corp. (“Cleo”)

We are legal counsel to Cleo. On December 8, 2024, pursuant to Part III of Division I of the Bankruptcy and
Insolvency Act (Canada), Cleo filed a Notice of Intention to make a Proposal (the “NOI”) which is attached as
Attachment 1 to this letter. Alvarez & Marsal Canada Inc. has been appointed as Proposal Trustee of Cleo.
As a result of the filing of the NOI, a 30-day stay of proceedings has immediately taken effect (the “Stay”)
and enforcement proceedings as against Cleo are stayed.

Certain garnishee summon have been issued by the Court upon Cleo and Royal Bank of Canada (“RBC”)
with respect to Cleo’s deposit accounts located at its RBC’s branch in Calgary, Alberta (the “Garnishee
Summons”). The Garnishee Summons are summarized as follows:

         1.       Morganic Blending Services Corp. - $152,436.18 – Court File No. 2401-10333

         2.       Vertex Professional Services Ltd. - $275,708.73 – Court File No. 2301-13984

         3.       Startec Refrigeration Services Ltd. - $155,799.02 – Court File No. 2401-11952

In the event RBC pays funds into Court pursuant to the Garnishee Summons and as a result of the automatic
stay pursuant to the filing of the NOI, all amounts received by the Court are required to be paid to Cleo.
Accordingly, please provide any amounts paid into Court as a result of garnishments issued against Cleo to
the following address:

         Gowling WLG (Canada) LLP
         Attention: Sam Gabor
         Suite 1600, 421 7th Avenue,
         Calgary, Alberta, T2P 4K9




Gowling WLG (Canada) LLP                   T +1 403 298 1000   Gowling WLG (Canada) LLP is a member of Gowling WLG, an international law firm

Dear Sir/Mesdames:

Re: Notice Of Intention to Make a Proposal Filed by Cleo Energy Corp. (“Cleo”) and
    Garnishee Summons made by Morganick Blending Services Corp. (“Morganic”)

We are legal counsel to Cleo. On December 8, 2024, pursuant to Part III of Division I of the Bankruptcy and
Insolvency Act (Canada), Cleo filed a Notice of Intention to make a Proposal (the “NOI”) which is attached
hereto as Attachment 1 to this letter. Alvarez & Marsal Canada Inc. has been appointed as Proposal Trustee
of Cleo.

As a result of the filing of the NOI, a 30-day stay of proceedings has immediately taken effect (the “Stay”)
and enforcement proceedings as against Cleo are stayed. Further, no new enforcement remedies may be
commenced or continued as against Cleo during the Stay, including garnishee summons issued by
Morganick as against Cleo.

Morganick previously obtained a writ of enforcement against Cleo and subsequently issued a garnishee
summons in the amount of $152,436.18 (Alberta Court Number 2401-10333) upon Cleo and the Royal Bank
of Canada (“RBC”) with respect to Cleo’s accounts with RBC. As a result of the automatic Stay implemented
by the filing of NOI, any funds that your office or your client receive from the Court of King’s Bench of Alberta
as a result of the aforementioned garnishee summons are required to be immediately repaid to Cleo. Please
advise if you or your client have received any funds from the Court and confirm that upon reception of funds,
that they will be paid either to Cleo or our office.

Should you have any questions, please do not hesitate to contact us.

Sincerely,

Gowling WLG (Canada) LLP




Sam Gabor

SG

Dear Jason Enns & Ruth James:

Re: Notice Of Intention to Make a Proposal Filed by Cleo Energy Corp. (“Cleo”)

We are legal counsel to Cleo. On December 8, 2024, pursuant to Part III of Division I of the Bankruptcy
and Insolvency Act (Canada), Cleo filed a Notice of Intention to make a Proposal (the “NOI”) which is
attached hereto as Schedule “A” to this letter. Alvarez & Marsal Canada Inc. has been appointed as
the Proposal Trustee of Cleo for its NOI proceeding.

As a result of the filing of the NOI, a 30-day stay of proceedings has immediately taken effect (the “Stay”)
and all enforcement proceedings as against Cleo are stayed. Further, no new enforcement proceedings
may be commenced or continued as against Cleo during the Stay, including the enforcement of any
garnishee summons.

As a result of the Stay, it is necessary that Royal Bank of Canada (“RBC”) immediately unlock Cleo’s
bank account no. 1222041, and any of Cleo’s other accounts with RBC (the “Accounts”), which were
previously frozen due to any garnishee summons served upon RBC, including the following:

    1. Morganic Blending Services Corp. - $152,436.18 – Court File No. 2401-10333

    2. Vertex Professional Services Ltd. - $274,708.73 – Court File No. 2301-13984

    3. Startec Refrigeration Services Ltd. - $155,799.02 – Court File No. 2401-11952

Furthermore, any garnishee payments currently in process are required to be immediately suspended
and all funds must remain in the Accounts. We also ask that any cheques issued to the Court of King’s
Bench of Alberta (the “Court”) based on any garnishee summons received by RBC, including those
above, be immediately cancelled. Finally, during the Stay, the Accounts may not be re-locked as a result
of any further garnishee summons served upon RBC.

It is critical that the Accounts with RBC be immediately unlocked today as Cleo is dependent on the
amounts therein to maintain its operations during the Stay, to meet its cash flow needs on a go forward

Dear Jason Enns:

Re: Notice Of Intention to Make a Proposal Filed by Cleo Energy Corp. (“Cleo”)

We are legal counsel to Cleo. We are writing further to our letter dated December 9, 2024 (the
“December 9 Letter”). All capitalized terms herein have the meaning set out in the December 9 Letter.

We have been advised by Cleo that it has been granted access to its accounts with RBC and we thank
RBC for taking those proactive steps.

Concurrently, Cleo advises us that as of the time of this letter, the most recent garnishee cheque sent
by RBC to the Court has not been cancelled and the applicable funds have not yet been returned into
Cleo’s Accounts with RBC. Our client has contacted the Court this afternoon which has advised it has
not received the cheque from RBC. The Stay requires that the garnished funds be available to Cleo as
no creditor is entitled to continue enforcement against the funds.

Cleo is required to make payroll this Friday. Cleo has determined that the aforementioned funds will be
required to do so and for Cleo to maintain its operations. As you can appreciate, it is now critical that the
cheque be cancelled and funds made available in the Accounts as soon as possible and no later than
end of day Thursday, December 12, 2024, so Cleo can make payroll Friday. If Cleo is unable to make
payroll, its oil and gas assets will be placed into significant jeopardy creating potential environmental
issues and risk of significant loss to Cleo and its creditors which are owed in excess of $23,000,000.
Cleo must make payroll to be in a position to maintain its oil and gas assets, continue carrying on
business and proceed forward with the NOI proceeding for the benefit of its stakeholders.

Based on the foregoing, we would respectfully ask that you please confirm today that the previously
issued cheque sent to the Court is being cancelled by RBC and the applicable funds will be available in
Cleo’s Accounts by end of day Thursday.
